_source line 8330 · 2026-05-20T01:46:40.325Z_

[Director 指令] [Patch 追加] Reviewer 指出 ICG Paradox 是论文级核心证据，请在主文 §5.3 ICG 段落中重点强调：

**ICG Paradox**：ICG 的约束客观上优于 SICA（Z3 编译率 99.4% vs 96.5%，冗余率 32.3% vs 42.9%，可比判别率 77% vs 81%），但准确率 Δ≈0。更干净、更多样的约束没有带来任何改善——because independently generated constraints still carry the same model's reasoning bias。

这是 "bottleneck is model bias, not constraint quality" 的最直接证据，应成为 "Why Methods Fail" 叙事的核心论据之一。

建议措辞：
"Paradoxically, ICG constraints are objectively superior to standard SICA constraints (99.4% vs 96.5% Z3 compilation, 32.3% vs 42.9% redundancy), yet yield no accuracy improvement (Δ ≈ 0, three seeds). This ICG paradox provides the strongest evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se, but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias."

这次不用验证引用。编译后通知我。

```text
49	
50	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, but achieves only 74.6\% parse rate (vs.\ 97.1\% for trace-conditioned SICA; Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), reflecting limited standalone formalization capability at the 7B scale. ICG produces near-zero average effect ($\Delta = -1.96$\,pp and $+1.47$\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\sim}$21\% degenerate scores on both datasets; all 34 discordant questions produced non-degenerate scores (vs.\ 20.1\% degenerate in the full sample), indicating that scoring failures stem from systematic pro-bias rather than missing constraints; 62.7\% of Contrastive constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3 (Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), explaining the degeneration mechanism (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
```

{
  "text": "[写作进展] Patch: ICG Paradox 强调 完成\n\n**更新内容**：\n\n§5.3 ICG 段落新增 ICG Paradox 论证（2 句）：\n- \"Paradoxically, among successfully parsed constraints, ICG produces objectively superior formulations: higher Z3 compilation (99.4% vs. 96.5%), lower redundancy (32.3% vs. 42.9%), and comparable discriminative rate (77.0% vs. 81.4%), yet yields no accuracy improvement across three seeds.\"\n- \"This ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias: independently generated constraints are cleaner and more diverse, but still encode the same model's systematic errors.\"\n\n叙事定位：ICG Paradox 现在与 Debate 的 discordant-pair 分析、Contrastive 的 trivially-satisfied 分析形成三方印证——三种不同机制都指向同一根因（same-model reasoning bias）。\n\n**编译状态**：✅ 成功，无 critical warnings。主文 8 页，总 18 页。",

}

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015jY2p8uzb3j4SciAWo4nau`</summary>

```text
49	
50	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, but achieves only 74.6\% parse rate (vs.\ 97.1\% for trace-conditioned SICA; Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), reflecting limited standalone formalization capability at the 7B scale. ICG produces near-zero average effect ($\Delta = -1.96$\,pp and $+1.47$\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Paradoxically, among successfully parsed constraints, ICG produces objectively superior formulations: higher Z3 compilation (99.4\% vs.\ 96.5\%), lower redundancy (32.3\% vs.\ 42.9\%), and comparable discriminative rate (77.0\% vs.\ 81.4\%), yet yields no accuracy improvement across three seeds. This ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias: independently generated constraints are cleaner and more diverse, but still encode the same model's systematic errors. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\sim}$21\% degenerate scores on both datasets; all 34 discordant questions produced non-degenerate scores (vs.\ 20.1\% degenerate in the full sample), indicating that scoring failures stem from systematic pro-bias rather than missing constraints; 62.7\% of Contrastive constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3 (Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), explaining the degeneration mechanism (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering

      "content": "\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, but achieves only 74.6\\% parse rate (vs.\\ 97.1\\% for trace-conditioned SICA; Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), reflecting limited standalone formalization capability at the 7B scale. ICG produces near-zero average effect ($\\Delta = -1.96$\\,pp and $+1.47$\\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Paradoxically, among successfully parsed constraints, ICG produces objectively superior formulations: higher Z3 compilation (99.4\\% vs.\\ 96.5\\%), lower redundancy (32.3\\% vs.\\ 42.9\\%), and comparable discriminative rate (77.0\\% vs.\\ 81.4\\%), yet yields no accuracy improvement across three seeds. This ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias: independently generated constraints are cleaner and more diverse, but still encode the same model's systematic errors. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\\sim}$21\\% degenerate scores on both datasets; all 34 discordant questions produced non-degenerate scores (vs.\\ 20.1\\% degenerate in the full sample), indicating that scoring failures stem from systematic pro-bias rather than missing constraints; 62.7\\% of Contrastive constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3 (Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), explaining the degeneration mechanism (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering",
      "numLines": 5,
      "startLine": 49,
      "totalLines": 87
    }
  },

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01CyeRQpHfxYEMCBC3fqB68D`</summary>

```text
49	
50	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, but achieves only 74.6\% parse rate (vs.\ 97.1\% for trace-conditioned SICA; Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), reflecting limited standalone formalization capability at the 7B scale. ICG produces near-zero average effect ($\Delta = -1.96$\,pp and $+1.47$\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Paradoxically, among successfully parsed constraints, ICG produces objectively superior formulations: higher Z3 compilation (99.4\% vs.\ 96.5\%), lower redundancy (32.3\% vs.\ 42.9\%), and comparable discriminative rate (77.0\% vs.\ 81.4\%), yet yields no accuracy improvement across three seeds. Moreover, ICG produces \emph{more} consistent outputs than trace-conditioned extraction: bootstrap-based constraint-set agreement yields $\kappa = 0.72$, exceeding the trace-level $\kappa = 0.44$ of standard SC. This suggests that model bias operates at the premise-understanding level, not merely at trace sampling---bypassing traces does not bypass the bias. The ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\sim}$21\% degenerate scores on both datasets; all 34 discordant questions produced non-degenerate scores (vs.\ 20.1\% degenerate in the full sample), indicating that scoring failures stem from systematic pro-bias rather than missing constraints; 62.7\% of Contrastive constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3 (Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), explaining the degeneration mechanism (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
```

Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering",

      "totalLines": 87
    }

The ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias.",
  "new_text": "Per-constraint-set MAX-SAT reranking yields $\\kappa = 0.47$ (Eff-$K = 1.93$), nearly identical to standard SC's trace-level $\\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$): even when constraints are generated independently from premises rather than extracted from reasoning traces, the resulting answers exhibit the same inter-rater correlation as trace-based SC. This demonstrates that the independence bottleneck is not an artifact of trace co-sourcing but a property of the model itself---the same model asked the same question converges to the same reasoning patterns regardless of the generation pathway. ```text
70	
71	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across three aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Self-Consistency produces $\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$). Diverse-Prompt achieves $\kappa = 0.40$ (Eff-$K = 2.21$): prompt variation does not break the underlying correlation. Debate achieves $\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.
72	
73	\begin{table}[t]
74	\centering
75	\small
76	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n = 204$). Debate achieves the highest Eff-$K$ but collapses accuracy. $\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}
77	\label{tab:kappa_generalization}
78	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccr@{}}
79	\toprule
80	Aggregation & $\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & Acc.\ change (pp) \\
81	\midrule
82	Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & (baseline) \\
83	Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & $\approx$\,0 \\
84	Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & $-$18.6 \\
85	\bottomrule
86	\end{tabular}
87	\end{table}

{
  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus",
  "section": "analysis",
  "old_text": "\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across three aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Self-Consistency produces $\\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$). Diverse-Prompt achieves $\\kappa = 0.40$ (Eff-$K = 2.21$): prompt variation does not break the underlying correlation. Debate achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor.",
  "new_text": "\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO. SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation can break. ICG is strictly dominated by SC: same correlation ($\\kappa = 0.47$ vs.\\ $0.44$) but lower accuracy (51.96\\% vs.\\ 56.86\\%), confirming that bypassing traces does not bypass the model's reasoning bias. Debate alone breaks through to $\\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor.",

### Tool result

_source line 8437 · 2026-05-20T02:11:46.863Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_014VFUVXka6NeGEBDZhJUw2G`</summary>

```text
37	\begin{enumerate}
38	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off (primary).} Fleiss' $\kappa$ generalization across three aggregation methods reveals a fundamental Pareto frontier: Debate reduces $\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (Eff-$K$: $2.0 \to 4.4$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp on FOLIO ($-23.0$\,pp on LogiQA); Diverse-Prompt preserves accuracy but cannot break the correlation floor. Twelve remediation strategies (nine prompt-level, three method-level) all fail, confirming that same-model aggregation cannot simultaneously achieve independence and accuracy (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
39	
40	\item \textbf{Independence Bottleneck and Diagnostic Tools.} Same-model traces yield Fleiss' $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ (Eff-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for $K = 12$), quantifying SC's own scaling ceiling: Eff-$K \approx 2$ means traces beyond $K = 12$ add negligible independent information for any aggregation method. The multi-candidate rate $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy provides a zero-cost deployment diagnostic predicting when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help (\S\ref{sec:condorcet}, \S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
41	
42	\item \textbf{Oracle-Extraction Gap.} Oracle constraints yield $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$). Self-extraction captures only 24\% of this ceiling, localizing the failure to extraction quality, not the symbolic approach (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
43	
44	\item \textbf{Regime-Dependent Pattern (directional).} A directional positive trend occurs in a narrow band ($\sim$50--55\% SC) in one model on one dataset (best $+3.43$\,pp, mean $+1.47$\,pp, none reaching $p < 0.05$). Three failure modes---confirmation bias, non-formalizability, error amplification---explain the regime boundaries (\S\ref{sec:main-results}).
```

</details>

  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus",
  "section": "introduction",
  "old_text": "\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off (primary).} Fleiss' $\\kappa$ generalization across three aggregation methods reveals a fundamental Pareto frontier: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (Eff-$K$: $2.0 \\to 4.4$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO ($-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA); Diverse-Prompt preserves accuracy but cannot break the correlation floor. Twelve remediation strategies (nine prompt-level, three method-level) all fail, confirming that same-model aggregation cannot simultaneously achieve independence and accuracy (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).",
  "new_text": "\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off (primary).} Fleiss' $\\kappa$ generalization across four aggregation methods reveals a fundamental Pareto frontier: SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$ (a same-model ceiling), while Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.4$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO ($-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA). Twelve remediation strategies (nine prompt-level, three method-level) all fail, confirming that same-model aggregation cannot simultaneously achieve independence and accuracy (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).",

_source line 8455 · 2026-05-20T02:12:17.816Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VYphyu3SoTQSjGFkYwsAG1`</summary>

```text
8	\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\kappa$ generalization analysis (\S\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. Diverse-Prompt achieves $\kappa = 0.40$ ($\approx$ SC's $0.44$): prompt variation does not break the correlation. Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.
9	
```

</details>

_source line 8488 · 2026-05-20T02:49:19.471Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01TA5dYWYGRkGyKWi6XseyPB`</summary>

```text
71	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO. SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation can break. ICG is strictly dominated by SC: same correlation ($\kappa = 0.47$ vs.\ $0.44$) but lower accuracy (51.96\% vs.\ 56.86\%), confirming that bypassing traces does not bypass the model's reasoning bias. Debate alone breaks through to $\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.
72	
73	\begin{table}[t]
74	\centering
75	\small
76	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO). SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$ (same-model ceiling); Debate breaks through but collapses accuracy. $\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}
77	\label{tab:kappa_generalization}
78	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccrr@{}}
79	\toprule
80	Aggregation & $\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\ (\%) \\
81	\midrule
82	Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\
83	Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & $\approx$\,55 \\
84	Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\
85	ICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\
86	\bottomrule
87	\end{tabular}
88	\end{table}

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The Director is orchestrating a Paper Agent to produce an EMNLP 2026 ARR paper (8pp main text limit, DDL May 25) on the "Independence Bottleneck." This session covers a series of Patch directives to integrate new experimental results and refine the narrative:
   - **Patch: McNemar paired test** — Add McNemar p-values to Table 4, discordant pair analysis to Debate paragraph, significance info to all method paragraphs
   - **Patch: ICG×LogiQA McNemar + framing** — Complete 6/6 McNemar matrix, add "only significant effects are negative" framing to Discussion
   - **Patch: Error Categorization** — Add 3 key findings to §5.3 + new appendix section with detailed breakdowns
   - **Patch: Constraint Quality** — Add cross-method constraint quality metrics to main text + new appendix table
   - **Patch: ICG Paradox** — Emphasize ICG's objectively superior constraints yielding Δ≈0
   - **Patch: ICG/Contrastive κ methodology correction** — Add ICG bootstrap κ=0.72 with proper labeling, exclude Contrastive κ=0.96
   - **Patch: ICG κ correction (0.72→0.47)** — Replace erroneous bootstrap κ with corrected MAX-SAT reranking κ=0.465
   - **Patch: ICG κ=0.465 into Table 5** — Add ICG row to κ generalization table, cascade "three→four" across intro/discussion
   - **Patch: F6 scatter plot** — Insert independence-accuracy scatter plot figure

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **SICA pipeline**: K traces → FOL constraint extraction → dedup → weighted partial MAX-SAT (Z3) → answer selection
   - **Independence Bottleneck**: same-model traces violate Condorcet independence; constraints inherit trace correlation
   - **Fleiss' κ and Effective-K**: κ=0.21–0.82 instruction-tuned, Eff-K=1.20–3.62 at K=12
   - **Independence-Accuracy Trade-off (HERO FINDING)**: SC/DP/ICG cluster at κ≈0.40–0.47 (same-model ceiling); Debate κ=0.15/Eff-K=4.44 but Δ=-18.6pp FOLIO, -23.0pp LogiQA
   - **McNemar's paired test**: 3/6 method-dataset conditions significant negative (Debate p<0.001 both, Contrastive FOLIO p<0.01); 0/9 SICA conditions significant positive
   - **Discordant pair analysis**: Debate FOLIO 60 correct→wrong vs 22 wrong→correct; LogiQA 64 vs 18
   - **ICG Paradox**: Objectively superior constraints (99.4% Z3 compile, 32.3% redundancy, 77% discriminative) but Δ≈0; κ=0.47 ≈ SC's 0.44, proving bottleneck is model property not trace co-sourcing
   - **Constraint Quality metrics**: SICA 42.9% redundancy, ICG 74.6% parse rate (vs 97.1%), Contrastive 62.7% trivially satisfied
   - **Error Categorization**: Debate fails when debate "succeeds" (2.93 vs 2.52 rounds); Contrastive 97.1% discordant contain negation; 15/79 cross-method overlap (Jaccard=0.19)
   - **Oracle ceiling**: gold FOL yields +14.22pp FOLIO, +11.50pp PW (p<0.001); self-extraction captures only 24%

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`docs/paper/analysis.tex`** (MOST MODIFIED)
     - Central analysis section containing Effective-K (Table 2), Supporting Diagnostics, Remediation Analysis with method interventions
     - Table 4 (method interventions) restructured with multicolumn headers and McNemar p-values:
       ```latex
       \begin{tabular}{@{}ll rc rc@{}}
       \toprule
       & & \multicolumn{2}{c}{FOLIO ($n = 204$)} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{LogiQA ($n = 200$)} \\
       \cmidrule(lr){3-4} \cmidrule(lr){5-6}
       Method & Target & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
       \midrule
       SC baseline & --- & 0.00 & --- & 0.00 & --- \\
       ICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96\,/\,$+$1.47 & .206 & $-$0.50 & .706 \\
       Contrastive & Scoring bias & $\mathbf{-9.31}$ & .007 & $-$4.50 & .160 \\
       Debate & Role diversity & $\mathbf{-18.63}$ & ${<}.001$ & $\mathbf{-23.00}$ & ${<}.001$ \\
       \bottomrule
       ```
     - Method-level interventions paragraph extensively expanded: ICG parse rate 74.6%, ICG paradox (superior constraints, Δ≈0), ICG κ=0.47 vs SC 0.44, Contrastive 62.7% trivially satisfied + all 34 discordant non-degenerate, Debate discordant pair analysis + 2.93 vs 2.52 rounds, cross-method Jaccard=0.19, "3/6 significant" 
     - Supporting Diagnostics: added 42.9% constraint redundancy
     - Table 5 (κ generalization) expanded from 3 to 4 rows with ICG:
       ```latex
       \begin{tabular}{@{}lccrr@{}}
       \toprule
       Aggregation & $\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\ (\%) \\
       \midrule
       Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\
       Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & $\approx$\,55 \\
       Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\
       ICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\
       \bottomrule
       ```
     - Independence-accuracy trade-off paragraph rewritten for 4-method comparison with "same-model ceiling" narrative and ICG strict domination by SC
     - Figure f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter added after Table 5

   - **`docs/paper/discussion.tex`**
     - Statistical evidence paragraph rewritten: opens with "The only statistically significant effects in this study are negative", details 0/9 positive significant + 3/6 method negative significant, McNemar discordant pair asymmetry, closes with "zero significant positives, multiple significant negatives—constitutes the strongest statistical argument"
     - Independence-accuracy trade-off paragraph: updated from 2-method (DP/Debate) to 3-method (SC/DP/ICG cluster at κ≈0.40–0.47 + Debate)

   - **`docs/paper/introduction.tex`**

   - **`docs/paper/appendix.tex`**
     - New section: "Constraint Quality Across Methods" (app:constraint-quality) with Table comparing Parse Rate, Z3 Compile, Unique/q, Redundancy, Discriminative, Triv. Satisfied across Standard SICA, ICG, Contrastive
     - New section: "Error Categorization of Method-Level Interventions" (app:error-categorization) with Debate discordant analysis (False 82.8% flipped), Contrastive discordant analysis (97.1% negation words, pro/con ratio 1.0 vs 0.68), cross-method overlap (15/79, 100% negation)

        "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThe regime-dependent pattern in \\S\\ref{sec:main-results} establishes \\emph{where} SICA succeeds and fails. This section investigates \\emph{why}: trace correlation quantifies the SC plateau (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), supporting diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}), and remediation analysis reveals a fundamental independence-accuracy trade-off (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Qwen3-14B in thinking mode reaches $\\kappa = 0.91$ ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$). The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.8\\text{--}2.2$), yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence, drives the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ (Qwen conditions with $K_{\\text{eff}} \\leq 1.30$ never improve) and sufficient SC accuracy (Mistral on ProofWriter with the highest $K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ but SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Even when both hold, sampling variance determines whether the constraint pool is discriminative.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s42) & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/f6_kappa_accuracy_scatter}\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$ (gray band: same-model ceiling), while Debate breaks correlation ($\\kappa = 0.15$) at catastrophic accuracy cost ($-18.6$\\,pp). ICG is strictly dominated by SC (same $\\kappa$, lower accuracy). Dashed line: SC baseline accuracy.}\n\\label{fig:kappa-accuracy}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostic Analyses}\n\nFour additional analyses confirm the mechanism underlying SICA's failure. Extraction error taxonomy (Appendix~\\ref{app:extraction}): 91.8\\% of constraints are technically discriminative, but their discriminative direction mirrors the majority answer; 42.9\\% of constraints are redundant across traces (Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), quantifying how co-sourcing from the same model produces near-duplicate formalizations. Removing the 8.2\\% non-discriminative constraints yields zero improvement. Constraint ablation (Appendix~\\ref{app:ablation}): on Mistral-7B ($n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter pooled), MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4\\% of predictions, with disagreements split nearly evenly (18 vs.\\ 17); the solver's weighted satisfiability computation adds no value over simple constraint counting because constraint weights carry information redundant with vote counts (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Per-domain, MAX-SAT provides a marginal edge in the intermediate-SC region (59.3\\% vs.\\ count-only 56.9\\%) but actively hurts in the Type~C regime (36.5\\% vs.\\ 37.2\\%), mirroring the regime-dependent pattern of SICA itself. Hyperparameter sensitivity (Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}): $K = 12$ is the only setting with $\\Delta > 2$\\,pp among five $K$ values, and only $T = 0.7$ among four temperatures yields a positive effect; both peaks coincide with relatively low SC baselines rather than reflecting hyperparameter effects per se. Entropy decomposition (Appendix~\\ref{app:entropy}): all SICA effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($\\Delta$ up to $+12.8$\\,pp in the intermediate-SC region), but the direction depends on the accuracy regime---high entropy signals correctable uncertainty only when the model has partial knowledge.\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test twelve remediation strategies: nine prompt-level spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) and three method-level (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). All nine prompt-level strategies fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\\% to 6.0\\% but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models extracting from stronger traces yield 0\\% discriminative constraints; stronger models extracting from weaker traces produce accuracy within $\\pm$1\\,pp of self-extraction. Only 1 of 4 temperature settings yields a positive effect ($p = 0.092$); multi-temperature sampling produces $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, but achieves only 74.6\\% parse rate (vs.\\ 97.1\\% for trace-conditioned SICA; Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), reflecting limited standalone formalization capability at the 7B scale. ICG produces near-zero average effect ($\\Delta = -1.96$\\,pp and $+1.47$\\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Paradoxically, among successfully parsed constraints, ICG produces objectively superior formulations: higher Z3 compilation (99.4\\% vs.\\ 96.5\\%), lower redundancy (32.3\\% vs.\\ 42.9\\%), and comparable discriminative rate (77.0\\% vs.\\ 81.4\\%), yet yields no accuracy improvement across three seeds. Per-constraint-set MAX-SAT reranking yields $\\kappa = 0.47$ (Eff-$K = 1.93$), nearly identical to standard SC's trace-level $\\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$): even when constraints are generated independently from premises rather than extracted from reasoning traces, the resulting answers exhibit the same inter-rater correlation as trace-based SC. This demonstrates that the independence bottleneck is not an artifact of trace co-sourcing but a property of the model itself---the same model asked the same question converges to the same reasoning patterns regardless of the generation pathway. The ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\\sim}$21\\% degenerate scores on both datasets; all 34 discordant questions produced non-degenerate scores (vs.\\ 20.1\\% degenerate in the full sample), indicating that scoring failures stem from systematic pro-bias rather than missing constraints; 62.7\\% of Contrastive constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3 (Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), explaining the degeneration mechanism (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B with McNemar's paired test. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds; $p$ for the first. {*}{*}{*}\\,$p < 0.001$; {*}{*}\\,$p < 0.01$.}\n\\label{tab:method_interventions}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rc rc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{FOLIO ($n = 204$)} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{LogiQA ($n = 200$)} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){3-4} \\cmidrule(lr){5-6}\nMethod & Target & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC baseline & --- & 0.00 & --- & 0.00 & --- \\\\\nICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96\\,/\\,$+$1.47 & .206 & $-$0.50 & .706 \\\\\nContrastive & Scoring bias & $\\mathbf{-9.31}$ & .007 & $-$4.50 & .160 \\\\\nDebate & Role diversity & $\\mathbf{-18.63}$ & ${<}.001$ & $\\mathbf{-23.00}$ & ${<}.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO. SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation can break. ICG is strictly dominated by SC: same correlation ($\\kappa = 0.47$ vs.\\ $0.44$) but lower accuracy (51.96\\% vs.\\ 56.86\\%), confirming that bypassing traces does not bypass the model's reasoning bias. Debate alone breaks through to $\\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}): methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \\citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO). SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$ (same-model ceiling); Debate breaks through but collapses accuracy. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nAggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\\ (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nDebate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\\\\nDiverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & $\\approx$\\,55 \\\\\nSelf-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\\\\nICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",

{
  "parentUuid": "1aef5337-c42d-4e5a-80ee-25d6de3af360",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThe oracle experiments causally identify the failure source. Holding all pipeline components fixed and varying only constraint source, gold annotations yield $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), while the best self-extraction captures only 24.1\\% of this ceiling. Because the aggregation mechanism is identical in both conditions, the gap localizes entirely to extraction quality.\n\nTrace correlation affects SICA through two channels. The \\emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity: Eff-$K < 2$ for many conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}) confirms that same-model traces violate the CJT independence requirement, explaining the SC plateau. The \\emph{indirect} channel operates through constraint quality: correlated traces produce homogeneous constraints, causing MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Appendix~\\ref{app:ablation}). The oracle result disambiguates: gold constraints applied to the same correlated traces yield $+14.22$\\,pp, proving that constraint homogenization---not voting diversity---is the proximal bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\\kappa$ generalization analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling: neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation breaks the correlation. Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \\emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\\geq 60\\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \\approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} The only statistically significant effects in this study are negative. Among nine SICA conditions, none reaches $p < 0.05$ (best $p = 0.092$); the four-seed replication yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$. In contrast, SICA degrades significantly on ProofWriter ($\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; $\\Delta = -2.50$\\,pp, $p = 0.011$). McNemar's paired test on method-level interventions sharpens this asymmetry: three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance (Debate $p < 0.001$ on both datasets; Contrastive $p < 0.01$ on FOLIO), all negative, with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\\to$wrong flips. Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$). This pattern---zero significant positives, multiple significant negatives---constitutes the strongest statistical argument for the independence bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} The method-level interventions that test the trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, Debate may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb; whether 14B+ or 70B+ models shift the Pareto frontier is an open question. The trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but its severity may vary with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors, external provers, and multi-model pipelines.",
        "numLines": 14,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 14
      }
    },

1	
2	\section{Prompt Templates}
3	\label{app:prompts}
4	
5	SICA uses domain-specific prompt templates for constraint extraction: one for mathematical reasoning (GSM8K, AIME) and one for logical reasoning (ProofWriter, FOLIO). Both share the same system prompt. The LLM is called with temperature $T{=}0.3$, max tokens $1024$, and structured JSON output mode. Up to 6 traces are processed in parallel via thread pooling, with 2 retries on failure (exponential backoff, base 0.5s).
6	
7	\paragraph{System Prompt.}
8	
9	\begin{lstlisting}
10	You are a constraint extraction tool. Output ONLY
11	valid JSON. Do NOT solve problems, reason about
12	correctness, or think step-by-step. Extract
13	constraints from the given trace exactly as stated.
14	\end{lstlisting}
15	
16	\paragraph{Mathematical Constraint Extraction (EXTRACTION\_PROMPT).}
17	
18	Used for GSM8K and AIME datasets. The placeholder \texttt{\{trace\}} is replaced with the reasoning trace at runtime.
19	
20	\begin{lstlisting}
21	Extract mathematical constraints from this reasoning
22	trace as a JSON object.
23	
24	Rules:
25	- type: "equation", "inequality", or "predicate"
26	- expression: human-readable, e.g. "x + y == 14"
27	- z3_formula: a single comparison expression in
28	  Python Z3 syntax
29	- source_step: step number where the constraint
30	  appears
```

```text
470	
471	
472	\section{Constraint Quality Across Methods}
473	\label{app:constraint-quality}
474	
475	Table~\ref{tab:constraint_quality} compares constraint quality metrics across the three method-level interventions on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Standard SICA extracts from reasoning traces and achieves the highest parse rate (97.1\%) but also the highest redundancy (42.9\%): nearly half of all constraints are syntactic duplicates across the 12 traces, quantifying the co-sourcing effect of same-model generation. ICG generates constraints directly from the problem statement without trace conditioning. Its lower parse rate (74.6\%) reflects the difficulty of standalone formalization at the 7B scale, though successfully parsed constraints compile at a higher rate (99.4\% vs.\ 96.5\%). ICG produces fewer unique constraints per question (24.9 vs.\ 39.3) with lower redundancy (32.3\%), consistent with the absence of trace-level repetition. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraint sets for each candidate answer. The format yields far fewer unique constraints per question (9.2) with zero redundancy (each pro/con pair is structurally distinct), but 62.7\% of constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3---they impose no logical tension and cannot discriminate between candidates. Only 51.0\% of Contrastive constraints are discriminative, compared to 81.4\% for standard SICA and 77.0\% for ICG.
476	
477	\begin{table}[h]
478	\centering
479	\small
480	\caption{Constraint quality metrics across method-level interventions (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n = 204$). Parse: fraction of extraction attempts yielding valid JSON. Z3 Compile: fraction compiling to Z3 formulas. Unique/q: mean unique constraints per question. Redundancy: fraction of constraints duplicated across traces. Disc.: fraction discriminative (different Z3 outcome per candidate). Triv.\ Sat.: fraction trivially satisfied (no logical tension).}
481	\label{tab:constraint_quality}
482	\begin{tabular}{@{}l rrrrrr@{}}
483	\toprule
484	Method & Parse & Z3 & Uniq/q & Redund. & Disc. & Triv.\ Sat. \\
485	\midrule
486	Standard SICA & 97.1\% & 96.5\% & 39.3 & 42.9\% & 81.4\% & --- \\
487	ICG & 74.6\% & 99.4\% & 24.9 & 32.3\% & 77.0\% & --- \\
488	Contrastive & 74.0\% & 94.4\% & 9.2 & 0.0\% & 51.0\% & 62.7\% \\
489	\bottomrule
490	\end{tabular}
491	\end{table}
492	
493	
494	\section{Error Categorization of Method-Level Interventions}
495	\label{app:error-categorization}
496	
497	We categorize discordant predictions (where the method and SC disagree) for Debate and Contrastive on FOLIO ($n = 204$) to understand why these interventions degrade accuracy.
498	
499	\paragraph{Debate discordant analysis.} Debate produces 60 correct$\to$wrong flips and 22 wrong$\to$correct flips on FOLIO. Answer-type breakdown reveals that False-labeled questions are most vulnerable: 82.8\% of False questions that SC answers correctly are flipped by Debate, compared to 63.9\% for True and 25.5\% for Unknown. Counterintuitively, failures concentrate not where debate breaks down but where it succeeds most thoroughly: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds (proposer-critic exchanges producing substantive updates) versus 2.52 for concordant ones. More extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints, consistent with the independence bottleneck---the critic draws on the same model distribution as the proposer, reinforcing shared biases rather than correcting them.
```

</details>

```text
70	
71	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO. SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation can break. ICG is strictly dominated by SC: same correlation ($\kappa = 0.47$ vs.\ $0.44$) but lower accuracy (51.96\% vs.\ 56.86\%), confirming that bypassing traces does not bypass the model's reasoning bias. Debate alone breaks through to $\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}): methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.
72	
73	\begin{table}[t]
74	\centering
75	\small
76	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO). SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$ (same-model ceiling); Debate breaks through but collapses accuracy. $\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}
77	\label{tab:kappa_generalization}
78	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccrr@{}}
79	\toprule
80	Aggregation & $\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\ (\%) \\
81	\midrule
82	Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\
83	Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & $\approx$\,55 \\
84	Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\

Debate alone breaks through to $\\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}): methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \\citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO). SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$ (same-model ceiling); Debate breaks through but collapses accuracy. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nAggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\\ (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nDebate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\\\\nDiverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & $\\approx$\\,55 \\\\\nSelf-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\\\",
      "numLines": 15,
      "startLine": 70,
      "totalLines": 88
    }

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01E3rvn9cBzqoaSEAeufW8iR`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The oracle experiments causally identify the failure source. Holding all pipeline components fixed and varying only constraint source, gold annotations yield $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), while the best self-extraction captures only 24.1\% of this ceiling. Because the aggregation mechanism is identical in both conditions, the gap localizes entirely to extraction quality.
5	
6	Trace correlation affects SICA through two channels. The \emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity: Eff-$K < 2$ for many conditions (Table~\ref{tab:independence}) confirms that same-model traces violate the CJT independence requirement, explaining the SC plateau. The \emph{indirect} channel operates through constraint quality: correlated traces produce homogeneous constraints, causing MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Appendix~\ref{app:ablation}). The oracle result disambiguates: gold constraints applied to the same correlated traces yield $+14.22$\,pp, proving that constraint homogenization---not voting diversity---is the proximal bottleneck.
7	
8	\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\kappa$ generalization analysis (\S\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling: neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation breaks the correlation. Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.
9	
10	\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\geq 60\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\leq 40\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method.
11	
12	\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} The only statistically significant effects in this study are negative. Among nine SICA conditions, none reaches $p < 0.05$ (best $p = 0.092$); the four-seed replication yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$. In contrast, SICA degrades significantly on ProofWriter ($\Delta = -2.83$\,pp, $p = 0.014$; $\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$). McNemar's paired test on method-level interventions sharpens this asymmetry: three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance (Debate $p < 0.001$ on both datasets; Contrastive $p < 0.01$ on FOLIO), all negative, with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\to$wrong flips. Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$). This pattern---zero significant positives, multiple significant negatives---constitutes the strongest statistical argument for the independence bottleneck.
13	
14	\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} The method-level interventions that test the trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, Debate may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb; whether 14B+ or 70B+ models shift the Pareto frontier is an open question. The trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but its severity may vary with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors, external provers, and multi-model pipelines.
```

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	The regime-dependent pattern in \S\ref{sec:main-results} establishes \emph{where} SICA succeeds and fails. This section investigates \emph{why}: trace correlation quantifies the SC plateau (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), supporting diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}), and remediation analysis reveals a fundamental independence-accuracy trade-off (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Qwen3-14B in thinking mode reaches $\kappa = 0.91$ ($K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$). The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ ($K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.8\text{--}2.2$), yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence, drives the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ (Qwen conditions with $K_{\text{eff}} \leq 1.30$ never improve) and sufficient SC accuracy (Mistral on ProofWriter with the highest $K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ but SC $= 39\%$ produces significant degradation). Even when both hold, sampling variance determines whether the constraint pool is discriminative.
10

12	\centering
13	\small

SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$, 4 traces each.}

39	
40	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostic Analyses}
41	
42	Four additional analyses confirm the mechanism underlying SICA's failure. Extraction error taxonomy (Appendix~\ref{app:extraction}): 91.8\% of constraints are technically discriminative, but their discriminative direction mirrors the majority answer; 42.9\% of constraints are redundant across traces (Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), quantifying how co-sourcing from the same model produces near-duplicate formalizations. Removing the 8.2\% non-discriminative constraints yields zero improvement. Constraint ablation (Appendix~\ref{app:ablation}): on Mistral-7B ($n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter pooled), MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4\% of predictions, with disagreements split nearly evenly (18 vs.\ 17); the solver's weighted satisfiability computation adds no value over simple constraint counting because constraint weights carry information redundant with vote counts (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Per-domain, MAX-SAT provides a marginal edge in the intermediate-SC region (59.3\% vs.\ count-only 56.9\%) but actively hurts in the Type~C regime (36.5\% vs.\ 37.2\%), mirroring the regime-dependent pattern of SICA itself. Hyperparameter sensitivity (Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}): $K = 12$ is the only setting with $\Delta > 2$\,pp among five $K$ values, and only $T = 0.7$ among four temperatures yields a positive effect; both peaks coincide with relatively low SC baselines rather than reflecting hyperparameter effects per se. Entropy decomposition (Appendix~\ref{app:entropy}): all SICA effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($\Delta$ up to $+12.8$\,pp in the intermediate-SC region), but the direction depends on the accuracy regime---high entropy signals correctable uncertainty only when the model has partial knowledge.
43	
44	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
45	\label{sec:remediation}
46	\label{sec:interventions}
47	
48	We test twelve remediation strategies: nine prompt-level spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) and three method-level (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). All nine prompt-level strategies fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\% to 6.0\% but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\Delta = -1.1$\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models extracting from stronger traces yield 0\% discriminative constraints; stronger models extracting from weaker traces produce accuracy within $\pm$1\,pp of self-extraction. Only 1 of 4 temperature settings yields a positive effect ($p = 0.092$); multi-temperature sampling produces $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
49	
50	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, but achieves only 74.6\% parse rate (vs.\ 97.1\% for trace-conditioned SICA; Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), reflecting limited standalone formalization capability at the 7B scale. ICG produces near-zero average effect ($\Delta = -1.96$\,pp and $+1.47$\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Paradoxically, among successfully parsed constraints, ICG produces objectively superior formulations: higher Z3 compilation (99.4\% vs.\ 96.5\%), lower redundancy (32.3\% vs.\ 42.9\%), and comparable discriminative rate (77.0\% vs.\ 81.4\%), yet yields no accuracy improvement across three seeds. Per-constraint-set MAX-SAT reranking yields $\kappa = 0.47$ (Eff-$K = 1.93$), nearly identical to standard SC's trace-level $\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$): even when constraints are generated independently from premises rather than extracted from reasoning traces, the resulting answers exhibit the same inter-rater correlation as trace-based SC. This demonstrates that the independence bottleneck is not an artifact of trace co-sourcing but a property of the model itself---the same model asked the same question converges to the same reasoning patterns regardless of the generation pathway. The ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\sim}$21\% degenerate scores on both datasets; all 34 discordant questions produced non-degenerate scores (vs.\ 20.1\% degenerate in the full sample), indicating that scoring failures stem from systematic pro-bias rather than missing constraints; 62.7\% of Contrastive constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3 (Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), explaining the degeneration mechanism (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.
51

70	
71	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO. SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation can break. ICG is strictly dominated by SC: same correlation ($\kappa = 0.47$ vs.\ $0.44$) but lower accuracy (51.96\% vs.\ 56.86\%), confirming that bypassing traces does not bypass the model's reasoning bias. Debate alone breaks through to $\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}): methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.

ICG produces near-zero average effect ($\\Delta = -1.96$\\,pp and $+1.47$\\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Paradoxically, among successfully parsed constraints, ICG produces objectively superior formulations: higher Z3 compilation (99.4\\% vs.\\ 96.5\\%), lower redundancy (32.3\\% vs.\\ 42.9\\%), and comparable discriminative rate (77.0\\% vs.\\ 81.4\\%), yet yields no accuracy improvement across three seeds. Per-constraint-set MAX-SAT reranking yields $\\kappa = 0.47$ (Eff-$K = 1.93$), nearly identical to standard SC's trace-level $\\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$): even when constraints are generated independently from premises rather than extracted from reasoning traces, the resulting answers exhibit the same inter-rater correlation as trace-based SC. This demonstrates that the independence bottleneck is not an artifact of trace co-sourcing but a property of the model itself---the same model asked the same question converges to the same reasoning patterns regardless of the generation pathway. The ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\\sim}$21\\% degenerate scores on both datasets; all 34 discordant questions produced non-degenerate scores (vs.\\ 20.1\\% degenerate in the full sample), indicating that scoring failures stem from systematic pro-bias rather than missing constraints; 62.7\\% of Contrastive constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3 (Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), explaining the degeneration mechanism (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B with McNemar's paired test. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds; $p$ for the first. {*}{*}{*}\\,$p < 0.001$; {*}{*}\\,$p < 0.01$.}\n\\label{tab:method_interventions}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rc rc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{FOLIO ($n = 204$)} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{LogiQA ($n = 200$)} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){3-4} \\cmidrule(lr){5-6}\nMethod & Target & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC baseline & --- & 0.00 & --- & 0.00 & --- \\\\\nICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96\\,/\\,$+$1.47 & .206 & $-$0.50 & .706 \\\\\nContrastive & Scoring bias & $\\mathbf{-9.31}$ & .007 & $-$4.50 & .160 \\\\\nDebate & Role diversity & $\\mathbf{-18.63}$ & ${<}.001$ & $\\mathbf{-23.00}$ & ${<}.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO. SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation can break. ICG is strictly dominated by SC: same correlation ($\\kappa = 0.47$ vs.\\ $0.44$) but lower accuracy (51.96\\% vs.\\ 56.86\\%), confirming that bypassing traces does not bypass the model's reasoning bias. Debate alone breaks through to $\\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}): methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \\citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO). SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$ (same-model ceiling); Debate breaks through but collapses accuracy. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nAggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & $K$ & Acc.\\ (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nDebate & 0.15 & 4.44 & 12 & 38.24 \\\\\nDiverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & 12 & $\\approx$\\,55 \\\\\nSelf-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & 10 & 56.86 \\\\\nICG & 0.47 & 1.93 & 10 & 51.96 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",
      "numLines": 88,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 88
    }

    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, but achieves only 74.6\\% parse rate (vs.\\ 97.1\\% for trace-conditioned SICA; Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), reflecting limited standalone formalization capability at the 7B scale. ICG produces near-zero average effect ($\\Delta = -1.96$\\,pp and $+1.47$\\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Paradoxically, among successfully parsed constraints, ICG produces objectively superior formulations: higher Z3 compilation (99.4\\% vs.\\ 96.5\\%), lower redundancy (32.3\\% vs.\\ 42.9\\%), and comparable discriminative rate (77.0\\% vs.\\ 81.4\\%), yet yields no accuracy improvement across three seeds. Per-constraint-set MAX-SAT reranking yields $\\kappa = 0.47$ (Eff-$K = 1.93$), nearly identical to standard SC's trace-level $\\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$): even when constraints are generated independently from premises rather than extracted from reasoning traces, the resulting answers exhibit the same inter-rater correlation as trace-based SC. This demonstrates that the independence bottleneck is not an artifact of trace co-sourcing but a property of the model itself---the same model asked the same question converges to the same reasoning patterns regardless of the generation pathway. The ICG paradox provides the most direct evidence that the bottleneck lies not in constraint quality per se but in the inescapability of same-model reasoning bias. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\\sim}$21\\% degenerate scores on both datasets; all 34 discordant questions produced non-degenerate scores (vs.\\ 20.1\\% degenerate in the full sample), indicating that scoring failures stem from systematic pro-bias rather than missing constraints; 62.7\\% of Contrastive constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3 (Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), explaining the degeneration mechanism (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. Debate failures occur not when the debate process breaks down but when it succeeds: discordant questions average 2.93 effective debate rounds vs.\\ 2.52 for concordant ones, suggesting that more extensive same-model debate produces more confident but incorrect constraints. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. Only 15 of 79 unique discordant questions overlap between Debate and Contrastive (Jaccard $= 0.19$), confirming that the two methods attack different vulnerability types while sharing a common root cause in the independence bottleneck (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.",
