  "old_string": "\\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\\kappa$ generalization analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling: neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation breaks the correlation. Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \\emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\\kappa$ generalization analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}) constitutes the primary finding. SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG form a same-model ceiling at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate breaks through only at catastrophic accuracy cost (Figure~\\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). This trade-off constrains \\emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure.     "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThe oracle experiments causally identify the failure source. Holding all pipeline components fixed and varying only constraint source, gold annotations yield $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), while the best self-extraction captures only 24.1\\% of this ceiling. Because the aggregation mechanism is identical in both conditions, the gap localizes entirely to extraction quality.\n\nTrace correlation affects SICA through two channels. The \\emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity: Eff-$K < 2$ for many conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}) confirms that same-model traces violate the CJT independence requirement, explaining the SC plateau. The \\emph{indirect} channel operates through constraint quality: correlated traces produce homogeneous constraints, causing MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Appendix~\\ref{app:ablation}). The oracle result disambiguates: gold constraints applied to the same correlated traces yield $+14.22$\\,pp, proving that constraint homogenization---not voting diversity---is the proximal bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\\kappa$ generalization analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. SC ($\\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling: neither prompt variation nor independent constraint generation breaks the correlation. Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \\emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\\geq 60\\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \\approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} The only statistically significant effects in this study are negative. Among nine SICA conditions, none reaches $p < 0.05$ (best $p = 0.092$); the four-seed replication yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$. In contrast, SICA degrades significantly on ProofWriter ($\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; $\\Delta = -2.50$\\,pp, $p = 0.011$). McNemar's paired test on method-level interventions sharpens this asymmetry: three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance (Debate $p < 0.001$ on both datasets; Contrastive $p < 0.01$ on FOLIO), all negative, with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\\to$wrong flips. Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$). This pattern---zero significant positives, multiple significant negatives---constitutes the strongest statistical argument for the independence bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} The method-level interventions that test the trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, Debate may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb; whether 14B+ or 70B+ models shift the Pareto frontier is an open question. The trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but its severity may vary with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors, external provers, and multi-model pipelines.",

This trade-off constrains \\emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results \\citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm} each bypass same-model self-extraction.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\\geq 60\\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \\approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method.",
          " "
        ]
      }
    ],
